//! Column worlds execute authored observers, including derived/count/refusal paths.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#121-column-read-intent — excluding a field from a projection must not produce a synthetic whole-row refutation.
use super::*;
fn reserve(out:&mut Campaign,cost:usize)->bool{
    if !out.reserve(cost){return false;}
    if out.column_observer_calls+cost>16384{out.unsupported.insert("column observers capped at 16384 call/world reservations; remaining value worlds not covered".into());return false;}
    out.column_observer_calls+=cost;true
}
fn discharged(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->bool{
    inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|kernel::implies(&super::super::columns::admission(&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table],inv.column.as_deref()),p)==Ok(true))
        &&!bound.schema.maintains.iter().any(|m|m.source==inv.table)
        &&!bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|kernel::dependencies(r).contains(&inv.table)))
        &&!bound.programs.iter().any(|p|ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(t,_)|t!=&inv.table)&&super::super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),&inv.table))
}
fn alternative(kind:&C,old:&Value)->Option<Value>{Some(match (kind.required(),old){
    (C::Text{max},Value::String(s)) if s.is_empty()=>if *max>0{Value::String("x".into())}else{return None},
    (C::Text{..},Value::String(s))=>Value::String(s.chars().map(|c|if c=='y'{'x'}else{'y'}).collect()),
    (C::Enum{variants},_)=>Value::String(variants.iter().find(|s|Value::String((*s).clone())!=*old)?.clone()),
    (C::Principal,Value::Id(s))=>Value::Id(if s==VIEWER{OTHER}else{VIEWER}.into()),
    (C::Int|C::Time,Value::Int(n))=>Value::Int(n.checked_add(1)?),
    (C::IntRange{min,max},Value::Int(n))=>Value::Int(if n<max{n+1}else if n>min{n-1}else{return None}),
    (C::Bool,Value::Bool(v))=>Value::Bool(!v),
    _=>return None,
})}
fn sample_rows(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,inv:&Invariant,row:&Value,presence:bool)->Option<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>>{
    let column=inv.column.as_ref()?;let kind=bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].columns.get(column)?;
    let old=match row.field(column){
        Ok(value)=>value.clone(),
        Err(_) if kind.is_optional()&&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].defaults.get(column).is_none_or(|d|matches!(d,snapback4_core::schema::ColumnDefault::Literal(Value::Null)))=>Value::Null,
        Err(_)=>return None,
    };
    let alt=if presence&&kind.is_optional(){if old==Value::Null{sample(kind.required(),0)?}else{Value::Null}}else{alternative(kind,&old)?};
    let mut out=rows.clone();
    let selected=out.get_mut(&inv.table)?.iter_mut().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok())?;
    if let Value::Row(fields)=selected{fields.insert(column.clone(),alt);}else{return None;}
    Some(out)
}
fn same_base_world(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,primary:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,alternate:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,key:&Value)->bool{
    bound.schema.tables.iter().filter(|(table,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()).all(|(table,definition)|{
        let empty=Vec::new();let rows=primary.get(table).unwrap_or(&empty);let other=alternate.get(table).unwrap_or(&empty);
        rows.len()==other.len()&&rows.iter().all(|old|other.iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok()).is_some_and(|new|{
            let (Value::Row(a),Value::Row(b))=(old,new)else{return false;};
            a.keys().chain(b.keys()).filter(|c|!definition.maintained.contains_key(*c)&&!(table==&inv.table&&inv.column.as_ref()==Some(*c)&&old.field("id").ok()==Some(key))).all(|c|old.field(c).ok()==new.field(c).ok())
        }))
    })
}
// Counterfactual field mutation must not break an authored creator's copies
// or recipient guards. Direct typed payload observations run independently.
fn coupled(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->bool{
    let column=inv.column.as_deref().unwrap();
    let table=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
    if table.rules.insert.needs_field_value(column,&table.columns[column])||table.update_rule_judged().needs_field_value(column,&table.columns[column]){return true;}
    fn definitions(value:&serde_json::Value,out:&mut BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>){
        if let Some(object)=value.as_object(){
            for tag in ["Const","Let","Assign"]{if let Some(binding)=object.get(tag)&&let (Some(name),Some(value))=(binding.get("name").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str),binding.get("value")){
                if tag=="Assign"||out.contains_key(name){out.insert(name.into(),serde_json::Value::Null);}else{out.insert(name.into(),value.clone());}
            }}
            for child in object.values(){definitions(child,out);}
        }else if let Some(xs)=value.as_array(){for child in xs{definitions(child,out);}}
    }
    fn fields<'a>(expression:&'a serde_json::Value,bindings:&'a BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>,depth:usize)->Option<&'a serde_json::Map<String,serde_json::Value>>{
        if depth>32{return None;}
        if let Some(map)=expression.get("Object").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object){return Some(map);}
        if let Some(name)=expression.get("Variable").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str){return fields(bindings.get(name)?,bindings,depth+1);}
        None
    }
    fn writes(value:&serde_json::Value,table:&str,column:&str,args:&BTreeMap<String,ir::ArgType>,program:&str,bindings:&BTreeMap<String,serde_json::Value>,defaulted:bool,found:&mut bool,coupled:&mut bool){
        if let Some(object)=value.as_object(){
            for tag in ["Insert","InsertIfAbsent","Upsert","Update","WriteTarget"]{
                let Some(write)=object.get(tag)else{continue;};
                let name=write.get("table").or_else(||write.get("target").and_then(|t|t.get("table"))).and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str);
                if name!=Some(table){continue;}
                let map=write.get("row").or_else(||write.get("patch")).and_then(|e|fields(e,bindings,0));
                let Some(map)=map else{*found=true;*coupled=true;continue;};
                if let Some(expression)=map.get(column){
                    *found=true;
                    if let Some(name)=expression.get("Variable").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str)&&args.contains_key(name){
                        let occurrence=serde_json::to_string(&Expr::Variable(name.into())).unwrap();
                        if program.matches(&occurrence).count()!=1{*coupled=true;}
                    }else{*coupled=true;}
                }else if defaulted&&matches!(tag,"Insert"|"InsertIfAbsent"|"Upsert"){*found=true;*coupled=true;}
            }
            for child in object.values(){writes(child,table,column,args,program,bindings,defaulted,found,coupled);}
        }else if let Some(xs)=value.as_array(){for child in xs{writes(child,table,column,args,program,bindings,defaulted,found,coupled);}}
    }
    let(mut found,mut unsafe_writer)=(false,false);
    for p in &bound.programs{let json=serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap();let text=serde_json::to_string(&p.program).unwrap();let mut bindings=BTreeMap::new();definitions(&json,&mut bindings);writes(&json,&inv.table,column,&p.program.args,&text,&bindings,table.defaults.contains_key(column),&mut found,&mut unsafe_writer);}
    found&&unsafe_writer
}
fn run_pair_variant(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,row:&Value,fixture:Option<&Engine>,trace:Option<(&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,&[histories::Step])>,presence:bool,out:&mut Campaign){
    if discharged(bound,inv){return;}
    if coupled(bound,inv){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: alternate value may break authored creator/guard coupling; only actual typed payloads and committed histories count",inv.target_label()));return;}
        (ir::ArgType::Optional(inner),v) if *v!=Value::Null=>one(e,inner,v,viewer)?,
        (ir::ArgType::List{of:Some(inner),..}|ir::ArgType::ListRange{of:Some(inner),..},Value::Array(xs))=>Value::Array(xs.iter().map(|v|one(e,inner,v,viewer)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?),
        (ir::ArgType::Image{..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:image"=>e.store_asset(viewer,&PNG,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.column_value(),
        (ir::ArgType::File{types,..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:file"=>assets::file(e,viewer,types)?,
        _=>v.clone(),
    })}
    values.iter().map(|(n,v)|one(engine,&args[n],v,viewer).map(|v|(n.clone(),v))).collect()
}

/// Fixture-free campaigns retain empty-start bootstraps and populated role worlds.
/// The two initial states share the existing 512-single/512-pair work envelope.
pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=0;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
}
/// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
/// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
/// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
pub fn fixture_observers(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    let first=campaign.violations.len();
    histories::initial_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    column_reads::fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
    if !campaign.generated_fixture {trusted_reads(bound,fixture,campaign);}
    histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    if campaign.generated_fixture {for witness in &mut campaign.violations[first..] {
        if witness.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace {witness.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;witness.scope="actual authored observation from a validated generated initial fixture; fixture initialization is not claimed application-reachable".into();}
    }}
}
pub fn operations(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    fixture_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    assets::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    let fixtures=match readback(fixture){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(e);return;}};
    let has_fixture=fixtures.values().any(|r|!r.is_empty());
    if fixtures.values().map(Vec::len).sum::<usize>()>1000{campaign.unsupported.insert("operation fixture matrix exceeds 1000 rows; provide a focused security fixture".into());return;}
    let mut viewers=histories::viewers(bound);
    for (table,rows) in &fixtures{for row in rows{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=row.field(c)&&!viewers.contains(p){viewers.push(p.clone());}}}}
    let eligible=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)&&!p.program.native).count();
    let quota=(8192/eligible.max(1)).clamp(1,512);let mut remaining=8192usize;
    for p in &bound.programs{
        if !matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.program.native{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: trusted/system/opaque operation",p.program.name));continue;}
        let target=bound.schema.visible_tables().find(|(t,_)|super::audit::mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),t)).or_else(||bound.schema.visible_tables().next()).map(|(t,_)|t.clone());
        let Some(target)=target else{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: no table population for generated arguments",p.program.name));continue;};
        let dummy=Invariant{table:target,target:"read".into(),column:None,predicate:"allow".into(),site:crate::Site::default(),policy:Some(RuleExpr::Allow),operation:Some(p.program.name.clone()),requirements:Vec::new(),syntax:None,unsupported:None};
        let cases=histories::fixture_cases(&p.program,&fixtures,&viewers,campaign);
        let mut exercised=0;
        'cases:for (variant,mut args) in cases.into_iter().enumerate(){
            let rows=if has_fixture{fixtures.clone()}else{match snapshot(bound,&dummy,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));break;}}};
            if !has_fixture{for (arg,kind) in &p.program.args {if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind&&let Some(row)=rows.get(table).and_then(|rs|rs.first())&&let Ok(id)=row.field("id"){args.insert(arg.clone(),id.clone());}}}
            for viewer in &viewers{
                if exercised==quota||remaining==0{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: operation matrix capped at {quota} calls to share the 8192-call budget across every operation; remaining arguments/viewers not covered",p.program.name));break 'cases;}
                exercised+=1;remaining-=1;
                let mut engine=match if has_fixture {fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())}else{engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.matrix",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]))}{Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: {e}",p.program.name));continue;}};
                let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut engine,&p.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: argument ledger unavailable: {why}",p.program.name));continue;}};
                campaign.attempted+=1;*campaign.operations.entry(p.program.name.clone()).or_default()+=1;
                if p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Query{
                    match engine.query(&p.program.name,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(_)=>{},Err(e) if e.code=="E_INPUT"=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: generated arguments rejected as E_INPUT",p.program.name));},Err(_)=>campaign.refused+=1,}
                }else{
                    match engine.mutate(&p.program.name,None,Context::new(viewer,args.clone()).at(NOW)){Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Failed{..})=>campaign.refused+=1,Err(e)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: matrix engine error {e}",p.program.name));},_=>{},}
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

fn fork(_bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,probes:Vec<Program>)->Result<Engine,String>{
    if !probes.is_empty(){return Err("fixture probes must be authored operations".into());}

//! Role authority is compared by effective gates, never by names like 'admin'.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#123-actionable-authority-advice — advisory grant-edge analysis, not a security proof or implicit exemption.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn probe_pairs(bound:&Bound,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[RuleTerm],equalities:&[(String,RuleTerm)])->Vec<(String,RuleTerm)>{
    let mut pairs=equalities.to_vec();
    if let Some(idx)=bound.schema.table(table).and_then(|t|t.index(index)){for (component,term) in idx.components.iter().zip(key){if let Some(column)=component.column(){pairs.push((column.into(),term.clone()));}}}
    pairs
}
pub(super) fn global_principal(bound:&Bound,table:&str)->Option<String>{
    let t=bound.schema.table(table)?;
    t.indexes.values().find_map(|index|{
        if !index.unique||index.components.len()!=1{return None;}
        let column=index.components[0].column()?;
        matches!(t.columns.get(column),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).then(||column.to_string())
    })
}
pub(super) fn membership_probe(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str)->bool{
    let RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..}=rule else{return false;};
    if table!=roster{return false;}
    let Some(principal)=global_principal(bound,table)else{return false;};
    let pairs=probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
    pairs.len()==1&&pairs[0].0==principal&&pairs[0].1==RuleTerm::Viewer
}
// @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — ownership is required for every supplied owned field;
// only an exclusively owned patch bypasses an unnamed row update gate.
fn write_admission(table:&snapback4_core::schema::Table,op:ast::WriteOp,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->RuleExpr{
    let touched=fields.iter().filter(|f|!f.spread&&f.name!="id").map(|f|f.name.as_str()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let owned_only=!touched.is_empty()&&!fields.iter().any(|f|f.spread)&&touched.iter().all(|f|table.written_by.contains_key(*f));
    let gate=match op{
        ast::WriteOp::Insert=>table.rules.insert.clone(),
        ast::WriteOp::Update if owned_only&&!table.update_transition=>RuleExpr::Allow,
        ast::WriteOp::Update=>table.update_rule_judged().into_owned(),
        _=>RuleExpr::Deny,
    };
    let mut owners=table.written_by.iter().filter(|(field,authority)|touched.contains(field.as_str())||op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&!authority.audience&&table.rules.update.needs_field_value(field,&table.columns[*field])).map(|(_,authority)|RuleExpr::Eq(if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{RuleTerm::Field(authority.owner.clone())}else{RuleTerm::OldField(authority.owner.clone())},RuleTerm::Viewer)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    owners.push(RuleExpr::Or(gate.disjuncts().iter().filter(|r|r.service_grant().is_none()).map(|r|(*r).clone()).collect()));
    RuleExpr::And(owners)
}
pub(super) fn membership_grant_risk(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,operation:&str,requirements:&[RuleExpr],op:ast::WriteOp,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->bool{
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Upsert{return membership_grant_risk(bound,roster,operation,requirements,ast::WriteOp::Insert,fields)||membership_grant_risk(bound,roster,operation,requirements,ast::WriteOp::Update,fields);}
    fn honors(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str)->bool{match rule{
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|r|honors(bound,r,roster)),
        r=>membership_probe(bound,r,roster),
    }}
    if global_principal(bound,roster).is_none()||!bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|honors(bound,r,roster))){return false;}
    let table=&bound.schema.tables[roster];
    let admission=RuleExpr::And(std::iter::once(write_admission(table,op,fields)).chain(requirements.iter().cloned()).collect());
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&global_principal(bound,roster).is_some_and(|column|kernel::implies(&admission,&RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::OldField(column.clone()),RuleTerm::Field(column)))==Ok(true)){return false;}
    let ordinary=substitute(bound,&admission,"$no-roster","$membership",None,None);
    bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==roster&&i.column.is_none()&&(i.operation.as_deref()==Some(operation)||i.operation.is_none()&&(i.target=="write"||i.target==if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{"insert"}else{"update"}))).filter_map(|i|i.policy.as_ref()).any(|p|super::operation::safe(p)&&kernel::implies(&ordinary,p)==Ok(false))
}
fn substitute(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str,column:&str,role:Option<&str>,target:Option<&str>)->RuleExpr{
    let known=|t:&RuleTerm|match t{RuleTerm::Field(c)|RuleTerm::NextField(c) if c==column=>target.map(|s|snapback4_core::Value::String(s.into())),RuleTerm::Literal(v)=>Some(v.clone()),_=>None};
    match rule{
        RuleExpr::Exists{table,key,index,equalities,..} if table==roster&&key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))=>{
            let pairs=probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
            let requested=pairs.iter().find(|(c,_)|c==column).and_then(|(_,t)|if let RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(s))=t{Some(s.as_str())}else{None});
            if requested.is_none(){rule.clone()}else if requested==role{
                if pairs.iter().all(|(c,t)|c==column||matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer)){RuleExpr::Allow}else{rule.clone()}
            }else{RuleExpr::Deny}
        },
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Id(seat)))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Id(seat)),RuleTerm::Viewer) if seat.starts_with("service:")=>RuleExpr::Deny,
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b)|RuleExpr::Ne(a,b)=>if let (Some(a),Some(b))=(known(a),known(b)){if (a==b)==matches!(rule,RuleExpr::Eq(..)){RuleExpr::Allow}else{RuleExpr::Deny}}else{rule.clone()},
        RuleExpr::And(xs)=>RuleExpr::And(xs.iter().map(|r|substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target)).collect()),
        RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>RuleExpr::Or(xs.iter().map(|r|substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target)).collect()),
        RuleExpr::Not(r)=>RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target))),
        _=>rule.clone(),
    }
}
pub(super) fn risk(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,column:&str,operation:&str,targets:&[String],op:ast::WriteOp,requirements:&[RuleExpr],dynamic:bool,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->Option<String>{
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Upsert{return risk(bound,roster,column,operation,targets,ast::WriteOp::Insert,requirements,dynamic,fields).or_else(||risk(bound,roster,column,operation,targets,ast::WriteOp::Update,requirements,dynamic,fields));}
    let table=bound.schema.table(roster)?;
    if requirements.iter().any(|r|!r.disjuncts().is_empty()&&r.disjuncts().iter().all(|b|b.service_grant().is_some())){return None;}
    let roles=role_values(bound,roster,column);
    let intents=bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==roster&&i.column.as_deref().is_none_or(|c|c==column)&&(i.operation.as_deref()==Some(operation)||i.operation.is_none()&&(i.target=="write"||i.target==if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{"insert"}else{"update"}))).filter_map(|i|i.policy.as_ref()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    fn role_guards(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,out:&mut Vec<RuleExpr>){match rule{
        RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..} if key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,v)|v)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))&&probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities).iter().any(|(c,v)|role_columns(bound,table).contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_))))=>out.push(rule.clone()),
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{role_guards(bound,r,out)},_=>{}
    }}
    let admission=RuleExpr::And(std::iter::once(write_admission(table,op,fields)).chain(requirements.iter().cloned()).collect());
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&kernel::implies(&admission,&RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::OldField(column.into()),RuleTerm::Field(column.into())))==Ok(true){return None;}
    let mut guards=Vec::new();role_guards(bound,&admission,&mut guards);
    let independent=kernel::implies(&substitute(bound,&admission,"$no-roster",column,None,None),&RuleExpr::Or(guards))==Ok(true);
    let mut ranked=roles.iter().filter(|r|targets.is_empty()||targets.contains(r)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let score=|role:&&String|bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(_,t)|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]).filter(|rule|kernel::implies(&RuleExpr::Allow,&substitute(bound,rule,roster,column,Some(role.as_str()),None))==Ok(true)).count();
    ranked.sort_by_key(|role|(std::cmp::Reverse(score(role)),role.as_str()));
    for target in ranked{
        for caller in std::iter::once(None).chain(roles.iter().map(|s|Some(s.as_str()))){
            if caller==Some(target.as_str()){continue;}
            let admitted=substitute(bound,&admission,roster,column,caller,Some(target));
            let admits=kernel::implies(&admitted,&RuleExpr::Deny)!=Ok(true);
            if !admits{continue;}
            let violates_intent=intents.iter().any(|p|kernel::implies(&admitted,&substitute(bound,p,roster,column,caller,Some(target)))==Ok(false));
            // Existing independently gated appointers may delegate different
            // roles. Independent intent can restrict a particular target role.
            if independent&&!violates_intent{continue;}
            if violates_intent{return Some(format!("{roster}.{column} assignment to '{target}' is not established by the independent delegation intent for {}",caller.unwrap_or("a caller without a role")));}
            for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for (effect,rule) in [("read",&t.rules.read),("insert",&t.rules.insert),("update",&t.rules.update),("delete",&t.rules.delete)]{
                let granted=substitute(bound,rule,roster,column,Some(target),None);let previous=substitute(bound,rule,roster,column,caller,None);
                if kernel::implies(&RuleExpr::Allow,&granted)!=Ok(true){continue;}
                if !dynamic&&!(matches!(effect,"read"|"delete")||!role_columns(bound,name).is_empty()){continue;}
                if kernel::implies(&granted,&previous)==Ok(false){return Some(format!("{} can confer '{target}', which adds {name}.{effect} authority not held by {}",roster,caller.unwrap_or("a caller without a role")));}
            }}
        }
    }None
}

pub(super) fn role_columns(bound:&Bound,table:&str)->BTreeSet<String>{
    let mut columns=BTreeSet::new();
    if let Some(t)=bound.schema.table(table){for c in ["role","kind"]{if t.columns.contains_key(c){columns.insert(c.into());}}}
    fn collect(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,table:&str,columns:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match rule{
        RuleExpr::Exists{table:t,index,key,equalities,..} if t==table&&key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,v)|v)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))=>for (c,v) in &probe_pairs(bound,t,index,key,equalities){if matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_))){columns.insert(c.clone());}},
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{collect(bound,r,table,columns)},RuleExpr::Not(r)=>collect(bound,r,table,columns),_=>{}
    }}
    let named=columns.clone();let mut observed=BTreeSet::new();
    for t in bound.schema.tables.values(){for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{collect(bound,r,table,&mut observed);}}
    let global=bound.schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t|t.indexes.values().any(|index|{
        if !index.unique{return false;}
        let Some(parts)=index.components.iter().map(|p|p.column()).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()else{return false;};
        let principals=parts.iter().filter(|c|matches!(t.columns.get(**c).map(|k|k.required()),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal))).count();
        if principals!=1{return false;}
        parts.iter().all(|c|matches!(t.columns.get(*c).map(|k|k.required()),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal))||named.contains(*c))
            ||named.is_empty()&&observed.len()==1&&parts.len()==2&&parts.iter().all(|c|observed.contains(*c)||matches!(t.columns.get(*c).map(|k|k.required()),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)))
    }));
    if global{for t in bound.schema.tables.values(){for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{collect(bound,r,table,&mut columns);}}}else{columns.clear();}
    columns
}

pub(super) fn role_values(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,column:&str)->Vec<String>{
    let Some(table)=bound.schema.table(roster)else{return Vec::new();};
    let Some(kind)=table.columns.get(column)else{return Vec::new();};
    match kind.required(){snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Enum{variants}=>variants.clone(),snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Text{..}=>{
        let mut roles=BTreeSet::new();
        fn collect(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str,column:&str,roles:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match rule{
            RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..} if table==roster=>for (c,t) in &probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities){if c==column&&let RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(s))=t{roles.insert(s.clone());}},
            RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{collect(bound,r,roster,column,roles)},RuleExpr::Not(r)=>collect(bound,r,roster,column,roles),_=>{}
        }}
        for t in bound.schema.tables.values(){for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{collect(bound,r,roster,column,&mut roles);}}roles.into_iter().collect()
    },_=>Vec::new()}
}

### 11.5 Reuse pure invalid-world judgments

Within one paired snapshot variant, final-exists validation rejects the same committed fixture independently of the later operation/viewer. Cache only this exact typed structural rejection for that present or absent world, retaining every observer-specific unsupported reason. Never cache an operation result, policy judgment, allocation/storage/resource error or successful observation. Actual admitted calls, counterexample checks and repeatability replays are unchanged. This avoids repeatedly constructing the same invalid world during check; it does not turn missing fixture coverage into a pass. The existing social offline-receipts guard remains unchanged and measures the recovered authoring path.


## 12. SEC3 day-two amendment

Implementer: SEC3, 2026-10-19. Commission: day-two fix list 3/4/5/8.
REL5 owns release transitions and compatibility; MIG owns backfills and staging
clock control. This amendment changes security analysis, not runtime authority.

### 12.1 Column read intent

`invariant table.column read only P` protects the value, computations over it,
value-dependent counts/existence/refusals, copied values, assets and delivery.
It does not forbid observing an otherwise public row's existence independently
of the protected value. Binding validates the column and preserves its identity
in every verdict and audit. Effective payload authority is row read AND any
owner-private field gate. Pure projections excluding the field may eliminate
that payload path; predicates, group/order keys, derived values and copied
fields remain observations. Unknown flow stays unproved. A synthetic whole-row
read cannot refute a projection-only column policy: column counterexamples
execute an authored observer in two schema-valid worlds differing only in that
column, with policy false in both. Outputs must differ reproducibly. Maintained
values are recomputed; column-sensitive counts/existence are included. Separate
obligations name unresolved raw-probe, wire, device and opaque flows. A direct
column theorem never silently clears those obligations. Per-column audit
reports effective audience plus operation/derived observers, not only its type.

### 12.2 Reproducible campaigns and reachability

Generated seed ingress contains only authored base rows/fields. Never re-seed
maintained targets or engine-recorded fields. Generated lifetime samples are
live at the campaign clock; principal fields vary independently. Rejected
snapshots are recorded as unsupported, not successful attempts. Existing
fixture histories instead fork the complete SQLite store and asset ledger into
owned isolated memory stores; captures, deadlines, maintained values, lifetimes
and actual media identities survive unchanged. No source store is modified.
Temporary asset directories belong to Store and disappear on drop.

Actors include all principal values in fixtures. Required/optional references
range over fixture rows, not only the first. Short histories visit first steps
breadth first before extending them under a reported work cap. Read and row
assertions are checked after each step. Write/operation assertions judge only
actual committed changes, correct insert/update/delete arms and pre-write
policy state; ambiguous multiple writes, implicit effects or unstable policy
need executed-effect tracing and remain unsupported. A finite trace is never
an induction proof for always or arbitrary histories.

Every counterexample has a machine-readable origin: authored trace from trusted
fixture, trusted-seed-only, or synthetic operation. Only the first establishes
application reachability from the recorded trusted starting state and fails
normal check/test. Seed/synthetic witnesses remain refutations of all-snapshot
claims, visible with --all and JSON, without failing acceptance. Unproved
reachability is never asserted unreachable as a mathematical theorem. Check
loads the same fixture base for reachable histories as test, without HTTP or
business-case execution. A stronger witness must replace a synthetic witness
for the same declaration. --all exposes all witnesses and never changes which
ones fail. No unreachable refutation may print pass/ok beside exit 1.

### 12.3 Actionable authority advice

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies authored caller operations that write role
or group grants trusted by later rules while the effective admission does not
establish independent authority to confer that particular grant. Include
self-signup roles and transitions where a caller can confer a role they lack.
A trusted service seat or an independently authorized administrator may appoint
other roles; holding an unrelated ordinary role does not establish delegation.
Advice names the operation, grant and gate and points to guide staging's
operator first-admin recipe. It is advice, never an implicit runtime refusal
or a proof of malicious intent. Legitimate open enrollment can be intentional.

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE requires an actually mutable/revocable current-role
population and a retained principal/grant branch lacking that role recheck;
cover read AND write branches. Immutable/static role populations do not fire.
N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE excludes public populations and caller-owned role lookups;
only an absent/hidden distinction crossing a narrower private audience warrants
advice. Every security note has a real source location and contributes to the
reported note count. Rule printing preserves connective precedence. Identity
429 responses include a conservative retry delay covering the rate window;
the existing limits and authentication semantics are unchanged.

### 12.4 Evidence

Qualification uses read-only copies of all ten wave-17 high lanes and their
spec-authored invariant sets, plus all 150 earlier schemas. Report caught,
partial and not caught separately; all-snapshot witnesses alone do not count
as a reachable detection. Preserve bad/fixed controls, note counts and source
hashes. The open qualification-gap ticket remains open for unresolved whole
proof/device/opaque obligations. No zero-hole claim follows from finite tests.


### 12.5 SEC3 reduction details

The direct column theorem is `(row_read AND owner_private_gate) => P` in the
existing three-valued kernel; deny-delete is never an admission for column
read. Whole proof still requires every observer obligation. This increment
uses dynamic column-world observers, not an unimplemented static dataflow
claim: pure projections can avoid a refutation, but do not acquire a proof by
absence of finite counterexamples. Census is existing point/scan/view/filter/
count/sum/exists, mutation return/refusal/copy, asset, sync, raw/derived/opaque
paths. Unsupported field flow remains unproved. Audit's `column_audiences`
maps every visible column name to its effective row-and-private-field rule;
operations and derived edges remain separately enumerated.

Column worlds use the same ordinary viewer, server clock and row id; the named
column has two different schema-valid typed values, and policy is false on its
respective image in each committed world. Every non-native authored query and
deterministic mutation is eligible; fresh ids, effects and incomplete/resource
results are declined. Compare typed logical data and authored refusal messages,
retain ordered arrays, repeat both observations to reject unstable outputs.
Rebuild maintained rows from authored inputs, never accept fabricated derived
or recorded fields. Required media substitutions without a coupled real ledger
remain explicitly unsupported. A primary observation from the exact trusted
fixture is a reachable observation; its synthetic alternate world establishes
value dependency, not reachability of an alternate application history. JSON
retains both worlds and explicitly states this distinction.

`AuthoredFixtureTrace`, `TrustedSeedOnly`, `SyntheticOperation` are the witness
origins (serialized in kebab-case). Status remains refuted for an all-snapshot
counterexample. Normal text labels seed/synthetic refutations non-failing and
hides their large witnesses; --all reveals them and does not change exit status.
Only authored fixture observations fail check/test. A reachable witness wins
over a synthetic witness for the same declaration. With no fixture, check uses
schema-only exploration and names missing application-reachability coverage.
Check discovers an explicit --fixture or project fixtures/; it uses an isolated
in-memory loader at the fixed campaign clock, never the development store.

Candidates sort by operation, argument value and principal. Enumerate bounded
Cartesian arguments including nullable enums and fixture references; interleave
operations for breadth-first first steps, then diagonal ordered pairs. The
512 first-step/512-pair and 1000-fixture-row caps are reported; no exhaustive
claim is made for unvisited combinations. Fixture asset directories are owned
by Store and removed on drop, including error returns. Histories judge direct
single-write net images only; multiple writes, dependency-changing and opaque
boundaries remain unsupported rather than fabricating write-boundary events.

The escalation advisory compares role privileges structurally, never by an
administrator role-name allowlist. Enumerate literals from a role enum or
role probes over a text roster; substitute each caller/assigned role into the
roster write gates and other table-effect gates. A possible ordinary assignment
that adds an effect the caller lacks warrants advice; explicit service-only
branches are excluded from this advisory cast, not from invariant proofs.
Literal assignment targets restrict the target set; dynamic values retain all
candidate roles. This is overapproximate advice: independent operation guards,
intentional enrollment and delegation still need specification review.
Grant-lifetime notes require a role/kind column with an authored update/upsert/
delete and a mutable role field; immutable seed-only populations are excluded.
Existence advice requires a private table lookup, an independent stricter read
policy and distinct later refusal; public and viewer-owned lookups are excluded.
Locations are exact column/rule sites where available, table sites otherwise;
the project-wide missing-intent note uses the first source's line 1.

Identity rate refusals on the existing identity routes use the existing
60-second WINDOW: Retry-After: 60, denied.retryAfterMs: 60000. This is a
conservative wait, not exposure of individual rate-key state. No limit changes.
Qualification inputs are ~/sb4-loop/wave17-repro/sec/<lane>/snapback/security.q
and read-only wave17 app copies; the report pins their hashes. The ten high
lanes are 006/011/013/015/019/021/027/040/045/048. Caught means an authored
fixture observation of the reported bad behavior; partial retains a relevant
note or synthetic witness with a missing affected path. Each of the eight real
highs is scored separately. All eight caught and no false refusals on 150 older
schemas is the target, never inferred from an incomplete run.


### 12.6 Disclosure and clock clarifications

A column observer mutation is declined if it can change the protected image,
policy dependency or an unresolved implicit writer before disclosure. A
pre-operation false policy cannot refute an authorized post-transfer return.
Column observers also run after committed history steps, preserving the
trusted initial fixture and authored prefix. All reference and principal
arguments use the same bounded fixture Cartesian cases; truncation of read
observer cases at 32 is named. Clock-only whole-row probes exclude columns.
Column tick coverage beyond the recorded campaign clock remains named.

All security fixture loading, probes, writes, paired deletions and policy
judgments use the same explicit campaign clock. Business tests keep their own
existing clock and store; security reloads trusted fixture files independently.
Raw policy probes respect both not-before and expire-at bounds. Direct delete
assertions judge removed pre-images against the stable pre-write state, never
surviving rows alone; ambiguous implicit removals remain unsupported.

Fixture forks copy already activated engine state without re-adopting a schema.
They use fresh entropy/minter and private assets, never shared live connections.
Ephemeral-hub fixtures require a separate isolated snapshot and are declined.
Structural privilege advice combines dominating safe operation requirements
with the actual insert/update/upsert gate and applicable owned-field patch gate.
A mutable roster alone is not enough for lifetime advice: require independent
current-role intent or a retained recipient column tied to a role literal
(judge, inspector, host, contractor, engineer; ordinary author attribution alone
is not a current-role contract). Deletion revokes even an immutable role field.

### 12.7 Stable judgments and direct payload witnesses

Unknown bound policy dependencies never authorize a mutation observer: such
column mutations require disclosure-boundary tracing. Pre-write assertions use
a transitive closure of maintained and cascade/set-null writes; a policy
dependency or target that may change implicitly makes the assertion unsupported.
The same rule covers insert, update, upsert and delete. Looped/opaque net images
cannot stand in for executed write events. Effective inserted authority includes
a defaulted role even when omitted from the source write.

An authored complete query returning a compiler-typed Row/Table or Page/Table
containing the protected row id is a direct payload witness at that query's
snapshot. Nested typed row returns retain their source-table identity. This
avoids inventing an invalid absent world by deleting a referenced parent.
Projected scalar/aggregate/refusal observations still require validated paired
worlds; a matching untyped id or value alone cannot attribute an exposure.

Check prioritizes actual fixture histories and column observers before searching
remaining synthetic counterexamples. It does not repeat the test-only operation
exploration matrix, which judges no additional policy. That matrix shares an
8192-call budget across every operation (1–512 calls per operation), reports
truncation, and preserves enum/reference/principal alternatives within that cap.
Required image/file operation arguments are actual uploads owned by the acting
principal, with ledger/scratch custody in the isolated engine. Unsupported video
and uncoupled column-world media identities remain named.

For escalation advice, a role-protected appointer is an explicit delegation
boundary; conferring a different role is not by itself evidence of escalation.
Independent role-value intent may restrict that boundary (for example a producer
must not confer legal). Ordinary role casts make service-seat equality false,
including inside operation requirements. Fixed literals still undergo the privilege comparison; ordinary enrollment
whose rules add no role-specific authority is not advised. Analyze caller-chosen
roles, defaulted inserted authority and owner-field upgrades; respect the
argument's actual enum choices. A fixed bootstrap that can appoint further
roles remains an actionable first-admin risk. Notes remain advisory; absence
of independent intent is not proof that a delegation matches the specification.

### 12.8 Runtime intent and independent worlds

Reference-hop intent is judged by the engine's own authorized RuleProbe and
three-valued verdict on an isolated actual snapshot. Hidden/missing references
remain Unknown, never false; snapshot-only evaluators decline those terms.
Temporal reference policies require a per-tick authorized replay and remain
explicitly unsupported until that path runs. Referenced immutable-column worlds
use fresh stores with copied verified asset ledgers and all authored base rows,
then ordinary seed validation/recomputed maintenance. They do not require a
legal delete transition. Every non-protected base field, capture, row identity
and row population must match before comparing observations.

Implicit stability includes source and member writes to row-keyed views,
maintained owner columns, through-parent effects and transitive cascade/set-null.
Only an actual direct delete can refute delete authority; commit-end expiry is
not relabeled a deletion by the caller. Defaulted upsert roles are analyzed on
the insert arm only; omitted fields are preserved on update. Revocability advice
requires an expanded write to a mutable role field or an admitted deletion,
not an unrelated display-name update.

### 13.6 Implementation qualification clarifications

Fixture-free execution retains both empty and generated initial states. The
empty state gets 64 single/pair history slots and the populated state gets 448,
sharing the existing total 512 singles/512 pairs plus observer reservations.
Empty-start traces retain AuthoredFixtureTrace because no synthetic initial
rows are assumed. Related grant/membership/ownership tables include every
sampled actor; payload tables need independent owner/resource rows and enum/
optional alternatives. Optional time/relationship values include null. Text
arguments include typed authored comparison literals; integer arguments include
bounds/changed values. Candidate ordering interleaves domains before truncation.
No new runtime allowance, query result cache or policy judgment cache is added.

Column paired observers prune only programs outside the transitive maintained,
through-parent and raw-rule observer closure; every retained experiment still
validates both worlds, fixed arguments, policy falsehood and repeatability.
Image/file alternatives upload into one fixture ledger fork shared by both
worlds. Once/immutable media uses a fresh validated store with that ledger.
Ambiguous/unsupported paths remain named, never proved. Initial disclosure
witnesses cannot be replaced by unrelated later prefixes.

The corpus survey reports original source exits separately from intent-quality
corrections on disposable copies: an omitted authorized service seat and a
private whole-row declaration over intentionally public occupancy totals are
declared-policy contradictions, not reasons to silently exempt seats or ignore
aggregate observations. The latter requires column privacy intent; the former
requires the intended seat explicitly. Preserve original hashes, exact witnesses,
corrections and both outcomes. Do not describe original-source refusals as all
green or infer an application vulnerability solely from these generated states.

The isolated production HTTP preparation also covers schemas with live
read-through. Preview population captures live in the disposable snapshot's
main database: activation may switch SQLite's temp store, which drops TEMP
tables. This preserves the existing before/after comparison; no live release
state or activation semantics change.

Typed payload recognition includes concrete rows already returned by an
incomplete page: incompleteness cannot undo a disclosure. Repeatability and
actual compiler-typed field identity are still required. Paired absence/boolean
comparisons retain their completeness restrictions. Every history absence world,
including a ledger reconstruction, revalidates cross-table final-exists before
comparison. Compact advisory headers make no claim that the whole check passed.
QRY owns changes to partial-return execution and hidden lookup semantics.

Implicit-write stability distinguishes affected tables from possibly deleted
tables. Plain updates do not fire on-delete cascades. Explicit deletes and
expiring written tables may delete; maintained targets and live read-through
retirement conservatively propagate possible deletion. Cascade actions continue
the deletion closure, while set-null propagates writes (and expiry if present).
This permits the upheld-appeal update intent of wave-18 033 without discarding
its appeal predicate merely because bouts declare an unrelated delete cascade.
No runtime deletion or cascade semantics change.

Expiring writes use the runtime's `is_expiring()` classification, including
inherited read-through deadlines and ephemeral lifetimes. Replacing an expired
identity may retire its predecessor and cascade approvals before later guards;
that dependency is never treated as stable just because authored `expire` is absent.

The language suite is still running. I’m also probing the raw-read path and generated-start validation for false positives beyond the supplied controls.

// Prioritize actually admitted relationship changes rather than filling the
// history cap with cross-products rejected by a recipient-role require.
fn relationship_candidates<'a>(bound:&Bound,p:&'a Program,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],cases:&[BTreeMap<String,Value>],fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign)->Vec<(&'a Program,String,BTreeMap<String,Value>)>{
    let writes=ir::write_tables(p);
    if !p.args.values().any(|k|matches!(k,ir::ArgType::Principal))||!writes.iter().any(|(t,_)|super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,t).is_empty()&&bound.schema.tables[t].columns.values().any(|k|matches!(k.required(),C::Principal))){return Vec::new();}
//! Server-authoritative lower row lifetime, independent of physical expiry.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.003#2-one-lifetime-predicate — every reader shares this half-open lifetime.
use super::*;

/// One opening is one system commit. Structural admission keeps that commit
/// inside the ordinary engine heap and event-count envelopes before any row
/// can enter the pending ledger.
pub(crate) const MAX_OPENING_EVENTS: u64 = 1_024;
pub(crate) const MAX_OPENING_BYTES: u64 = 16 << 20;

impl Table {
    /// Reads must classify/filter stored images before public actual-size
    /// accounting whenever either lifetime edge can hide one.
    pub fn read_lifetime_sensitive(&self) -> bool {
        self.not_before.is_some() || self.is_expiring()
    }

    pub fn opens_at(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        self.not_before
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|field| match row.field(field) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                Ok(_) => None,
                Err(_) => match self.defaults.get(field) {
                    Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(Value::Int(at))) => Some(*at),
                    _ => None,
                },
            })
    }
    pub fn opened(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.opens_at(row).is_none_or(|at| at <= now)
    }
    pub fn live(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.opened(row, now) && !self.expired(row, now)
    }
    pub fn opening_index(&self) -> Option<(&str, &Index)> {
        let column = self.not_before.as_deref()?;
        self.indexes
            .iter()
            .find(|(_, index)| {
                index.is_plain() && index.columns().first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
            })
            .map(|(name, index)| (name.as_str(), index))
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let timed = |name: &str| {
        schema
            .table(name)
            .is_some_and(|table| table.not_before.is_some())
    };
    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
        let path = format!("tables.{name}");
        if table.not_before.is_some() && table.ephemeral.is_some() {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.ephemeral"), message:
                "ephemeral: not before cannot appear on an ephemeral table; keep the scheduled payload in a durable table".into() });
        }
        if let Some(through) = &table.through
            && (table.not_before.is_some() || timed(&through.parent))
        {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message:
                format!("not before cannot compose with read through: {name} and {} need one captured opening boundary; use an explicit durable table", through.parent) });
        }
        if table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(Sync::is_union) {
            let mut scopes = vec![name.as_str()];
            scopes.extend(
                table
                    .sync
                    .as_ref()
                    .into_iter()
                    .flat_map(Sync::arms)
                    .filter_map(|(audience, _)| match audience {
                        Audience::Target { table, .. } => Some(table.as_str()),
                        _ => None,
                    }),
            );
            if let Some(scope) = scopes.into_iter().find(|scope| timed(scope)) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.sync"), message:
                    format!("union audiences cannot use a time audience: {scope} has `not before`; use `online only`") });
            }
        }
        if table.not_before.is_some() {
            let own = table
                .sync
                .as_ref()
                .map_or(0, |sync| sync.arms().count() as u64)
                + table
                    .written_by
                    .values()
                    .filter(|authority| !authority.audience)
                    .count() as u64;
            let memberships = schema.tables.values().flat_map(|content| content.sync.iter().flat_map(Sync::arms))
                .filter(|(audience, _)| matches!(audience, Audience::Target { table: target, .. } if target == name))
                .count() as u64;
            let events = own.saturating_add(memberships);
            let owner_slots = table
                .written_by
                .values()
                .filter(|authority| !authority.audience)
                .count() as u64;
            let mut read_probes = Vec::new();
            crate::engine::rules::probe_sites(&table.rules.read, &mut read_probes);
            // The opening row is decoded before publication, including for an
            // online-only table which emits no event. A join carries a subset
            // of that row's bounded fields plus less framing than this same
            // envelope. Reserve at least the base decode and one envelope for
            // every possible event.
            let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table)
                .saturating_mul(events.max(1));
            if events > MAX_OPENING_EVENTS || bytes > MAX_OPENING_BYTES {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message: format!(
                    "not before opening fanout is too large for one bounded publication commit ({events} events, {bytes} reserved bytes); split this audience into smaller tables"
                ) });
            }
            // Opening runs as the system principal, so its recipient-specific
            // private-field projection cannot safely borrow the caller's
            // bounded authorization walk or scratch. Keep that projection
            // local to the opening row; ordinary rows may still use witnesses.
            if owner_slots > 0 && !read_probes.is_empty() {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message: format!(
                    "not before with private `written by` fields needs a probe-free read rule so opening publication has no unbounded witness walk; use local field comparisons, declare the fields readable by audience, or remove `not before`"
                ) });
            }
        }
    }
    for (position, maintain) in schema.maintains.iter().enumerate() {
        let timed_row_key = maintain
            .row_key
            .as_ref()
            .is_some_and(|row_key| timed(&row_key.table));
        if timed(&maintain.source) || timed(&maintain.target) || timed_row_key {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("maintains[{position}]"), message:
                format!("maintain {} cannot use `not before` on its source, target, or row-key member table until boundary recounts are supported", maintain.target) });
        }
    }
    for timeline in &schema.timelines {
        if let Some(table) = timeline
            .event_tables()
            .into_iter()
            .find(|table| timed(table))
        {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue {
                path: format!("timelines.{}", timeline.name),
                message: format!(
                    "timeline {} cannot map time-audience table {table}; query the table directly",
                    timeline.name
                ),
            });
        }
    }
}
    let column=inv.column.as_deref().unwrap();
    let kind=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].columns[column];
    let mut other=match sample_rows(bound,rows,inv,row,presence){
        Some(other)=>other,
        None if kind.contains_asset()&&fixture.is_some()=>{

/// Schema-only engine challenges. Successful synthetic operations refute the
/// declared table-authority contract, not application-operation reachability.
pub fn run(bound:&Bound)->Campaign{run_selected(bound,None)}
/// `check` searches unproved direct obligations; unsearched blockers remain
/// explicitly unproved. Default `test` passes None and runs the whole set.
pub fn run_selected(bound:&Bound,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>)->Campaign{run_selected_after(bound,sites,Campaign::default())}
/// Continue after trusted observations, preserving one set of work budgets.
pub fn run_selected_after(bound:&Bound,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>,mut out:Campaign)->Campaign{
    // A direct component theorem needs no repeated concrete search for that
    // same component. Indirect observers, temporal reads and actual authored
    // operations below still run; the whole declaration remains unproved.
    let discharged=super::analyze(bound).invariants.into_iter()
        .filter(|v|v.direct_rule==Status::Proved&&v.invariant.operation.is_none()
            && (matches!(v.invariant.target.as_str(),"read"|"always"|"insert"|"delete")
                || matches!(v.invariant.target.as_str(),"update"|"write")&&bound.schema.tables[&v.invariant.table].written_by.is_empty()))
        .map(|v|v.invariant.site.to_string()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let mut assertions=bound.invariants.clone();
    for inv in &bound.invariants {
        if inv.target=="read"&&inv.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|c!="id"){continue;}
        if inv.operation.is_some()||matches!(inv.target.as_str(),"read"|"always"){continue;}
        for p in &bound.programs {if p.program.kind!=ProgramKind::Mutation{continue;}
            let relevant=ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(table,kind)|table==&inv.table&&(inv.target=="write"||inv.target==*kind||*kind=="upsert"));
            if relevant{let mut op=inv.clone();op.operation=Some(p.program.name.clone());assertions.push(op);}
        }
    }
    for inv in &assertions{
        if out.exhausted(){break;}
        if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.reachability.is_reachable()){continue;}
        if inv.target=="read"&&inv.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|c!="id"){continue;}
        if sites.is_some_and(|sites|!sites.contains(&inv.site.to_string())){continue;}
        if inv.operation.is_none()&&discharged.contains(&inv.site.to_string()){continue;}
        if let Err(why)=operation_preflight(bound,inv){out.unsupported.insert(why);continue;}
        let mut found=false;let mut seen=BTreeSet::new();
        let combinations=bound.schema.tables.values().flat_map(|t|t.columns.values()).filter_map(|k|if let C::Enum{variants}=k.required(){Some(variants.len())}else{None}).fold(1usize,usize::saturating_mul).max(24);
        if combinations>512{out.unsupported.insert("schema enum Cartesian matrix exceeds 512 samples; remaining tuples are not covered".into());}
        for sample in 0..=combinations.min(512) {
            if !out.reserve(1){break;}
            let variant=sample.saturating_sub(1);
            let mut rows=match snapshot(bound,inv,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);break;}};
            // Try explicit policy field literals before the unchanged enum
            // matrix. A value such as status='cancelled' need not wait behind
            // hundreds of unrelated enum tuples. This is only a candidate;
            // ordinary validation and engine replay still decide refutation.
            if sample==0 {
                fn pin(rule:&RuleExpr,row:&mut Value)->bool{match rule{
                    RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Literal(v))|RuleExpr::Ne(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Literal(v))|
                    RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Field(f))|RuleExpr::Ne(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Field(f))=>{
                        if let Value::Row(fields)=row&&fields.contains_key(f){fields.insert(f.clone(),v.clone());true}else{false}
                    },
                    RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().fold(false,|any,r|pin(r,row)||any),
                    RuleExpr::Not(r)=>pin(r,row),_=>false,
                }}
                if !inv.policy.as_ref().zip(rows.get_mut(&inv.table).and_then(|rs|rs.first_mut())).is_some_and(|(p,row)|pin(p,row)){continue;}
            }
            let candidate_args=inv.operation.as_ref().and_then(|op|bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==op)).and_then(|p|arguments(&p.program.args,variant));
            // Pruned dependencies can repeat after unrelated schema enum digits.
            // Identical seed/typed-argument candidates are one search input.
            if !seen.insert(serde_json::to_string(&(&rows,&candidate_args)).unwrap()){continue;}
            let mut seeded=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Get(Read{site:"security read".into(),table:inv.table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),key:vec![Expr::Literal(id(&inv.table))],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed}))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
            let rows=match readback(&seeded){Ok(r)=>r,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
            let Some(row)=rows.get(&inv.table).and_then(|r|r.first())else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: no generated ordinary row",inv.table));break;};
            for viewer in [VIEWER,OTHER,"account:security-no-role","service:security"]{
                if !out.reserve(1){break;}
                if inv.target=="always" {
                    match policy(bound,inv,&rows,viewer,None,row){
                        Ok(false)=>{out.attempted+=1;out.admitted+=1;out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:Reachability::TrustedSeedOnly,history:Vec::new(),observations:None,invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:BTreeMap::new(),viewer:viewer.into(),operation:"$security.seed_at".into(),effect:"always".into(),row:row.clone(),old:None,supporting_rows:rows.clone(),rule_path:"trusted seed ingress; all typed and final-exists constraints rechecked".into(),scope:"actual seed_at commit/read-back; ordinary application reachability is not claimed".into()});found=true;},
                        Ok(true)=>{out.attempted+=1;},Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);}
                    }if found{break;}continue;
                }
                if let Some(operation)=&inv.operation {
                    let Some(p)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==operation)else{continue;};
                    let permitted=match policy(bound,inv,&rows,viewer,None,row){Ok(p)=>p,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: operation intent not judged: {why}"));continue;}};
                    let Some(args)=arguments(&p.program.args,variant)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: arguments need fixture assets or unsupported samples"));continue;};
                    if !out.reserve(1){break;}
                    out.attempted+=1;
                    let mut seeded=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$security.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(e)=>{out.unsupported.insert(e);continue;}};
                    let args=match materialize_arguments(&mut seeded,&p.program.args,&args,viewer){Ok(a)=>a,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{operation}: argument ledger unavailable: {why}"));continue;}};
## 4. Counterexamples and generated tests

Generate deterministic challenges from schema types, policy atoms and fixture
rows. Use in-memory stores; never leave SQLite sidecars under target. Vary the
viewer across owner/other-user/other-role/revoked-role/unauthenticated classes,
policy literals, principal arguments and absent optional values. Existing role
rows and retained grant rows form revoked-role snapshots. Do not call an
arbitrary consistent snapshot a *reachable* application-operation sequence.

Concrete counterexamples must replay the engine's actual rule admission and
show the policy false, recording viewer, effect, row, supporting rows and rule
path. The absence of a concrete witness when implication fails is **unproved**.
Generated tests are a separate executable check, run by default `test`, with
coverage counts and named unsupported cases. They use isolated state so they
cannot corrupt the fixture business tests. For operations, derive arguments
from fixtures and schema, substitute other principals and roles, and compare
admitted effects/observations against the declared intent. Merely succeeding
as another user is not a failure when policy permits that operation. Signed-out
network admission cannot be tested by inventing a signed-in principal: label
that boundary separately. No finite campaign promises arbitrary sequence
coverage. `test` fails on replayed violations; unproved coverage is explicit.

## 5. Exposure report
### 10.1 Separate observer obligations

Report individual observer obligations as well as the whole verdict. A proved
component never clears another unresolved component. Ordinary point, scan,
planned union/projection, count, sum and existence reads consume authorized
rows; pure computation over those rows cannot reveal an unauthorized payload.
The reduction concerns logical data, not the already excluded examined-count,
meter, physical timing or the separately unproved global wire activity path.
Context changes, native programs and external workers are never pure reads.

For a maintained count or sum with ordinary bare group keys, pull the derived
read predicate back to the source row by substituting its group columns.
Prove that predicate implies the source invariant using the existing exhaustive
kernel. Do not assume the source read rule protects an explicitly broader
aggregate. Lost source fields, value-dependent derived rules, aliases,
row-keyed/private/captured maintenance, temporal contributions and downstream
derived observers without a checked substitution stay unproved with the exact
edge and an author repair. A filter can narrow contributors, but is not used
as an assumption in this first reduction. No maintenance consistency axiom is
added to the propositional kernel.

Generate paired in-memory engine worlds, identical except for one protected
row, only when both worlds seed and validate and the privacy policy is false
in both. Execute the same authored operation with identical arguments/viewer
and clock. Different returned data or refusal code/message is a concrete
logical-existence counterexample; ignore sequence numbers, receipts, meters
and timing. Keep both executions and inputs in the witness. An engine/storage
error or unsupported policy evaluation cannot refute. A passing finite paired
campaign proves nothing. Maintenance is rebuilt independently in each world;
never seed a fabricated derived value. Unsupported referential/final-existence
worlds are declined. This tests count/sum/existence, mutation returns and
collision/refusal observations without guessing which successful call is bad.

### 10.2 Temporal intent
//! A one-hop equality in an online-only table's read rule is the probe of the
//! row that decides it: `.goal.owner = viewer` reads exactly as
//! `exists goals[.goal, owner = viewer]`. Both are false when the reference
//! is null or names no row, and equal otherwise, so the lowering admits the
//! same readers under `and`, `or` and `not`.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#14.3 — wave 4 #8: a hop inside `or` compiles as the probe the diagnostic printed.
use super::*;
use crate::Site;

/// Lower the read rules that hop but do not spell a membership path (those
/// stay `read through`). Synced tables keep `E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP`: sync needs
/// the reader on the row, and its rewrite copies it there.
pub(super) fn lower(tables: &mut BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>) {
    let mut lowered = Vec::new();
    for (name, info) in tables.iter() {
        if info.decl.sync.is_some() || info.decl.ephemeral.is_some() || info.maintained.is_some() { continue; }
        let Some(reference) = super::reference(info) else { continue };
        if paths::declares_membership(info, &reference, tables) { continue; }
        let Some(position) = info.decl.rules.iter().position(|rule| rule.effect == Effect::Read && rule.bindings.is_empty()) else { continue };
        let mut value = info.decl.rules[position].value.clone();
        if rewrite(&mut value, info, tables) { lowered.push((name.clone(), position, value)); }
    }
    for (name, position, value) in lowered {
        if let Some(info) = tables.get_mut(&name) { info.decl.rules[position].value = value; }
    }
}

fn rewrite(expr: &mut Expr, info: &TableInfo, tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>) -> bool {
    match expr {
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" || op == "or" => {
            let a = rewrite(left, info, tables);
            let b = rewrite(right, info, tables);
            a || b
        }
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => rewrite(value, info, tables),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, site } if op == "=" => {
            let (hop, other) = if hop(left).is_some() { (left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()) } else { (right.as_ref(), left.as_ref()) };
            let Some((reference, field)) = hop_parts(hop) else { return false };
            match probe(info, tables, reference, field, other, site) { Some(probe) => { *expr = probe; true } None => false }
        }
        Expr::Member { .. } => {
            let site = expr.site().clone();
            let Some((reference, field)) = hop_parts(expr) else { return false };
            let truth = Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(true), site: site.clone() };
            match probe(info, tables, reference, field, &truth, &site) { Some(probe) => { *expr = probe; true } None => false }

exec

#[test]
fn aliases_defaults_and_deprecated_inputs_preserve_column_challenges_and_audit() {
 // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#121-column-read-intent — compatibility forms keep the same disclosure obligations.
 for fixed in [false, true] {
  let source = SOURCE.replace("invariant reports.read only", "invariant reports.body read only")
   .replace("query inspect(report: reports):", "query inspect(report: reports, enabled: bool default true, old: text <=20 deprecated):")
   .replace("  return reports[report]", "  require enabled else DISABLED\n  return reports[report]")
   + "alias legacyInspect = inspect\n";
  let source = if fixed { source.replace("read <- exists grants[viewer]", "read <- exists grants[viewer] and exists roles[viewer, role = 'inspector']") } else { source };
  let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
  let report=security::analyze(&b);
  let exposure=report.exposures.iter().find(|e|e.table=="reports").unwrap();
  for name in ["inspect (query)", "legacyInspect (query)"] { assert!(exposure.operations.iter().any(|op|op==name),"{exposure:?}"); }
  let now=1_800_000_000_000;
  let mut fixture=Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(),b.schema.clone(),b.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),now).unwrap();
  fixture.seed_at([
   ("roles".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("role".into())),("principal",Value::Id("dev:fixture-only".into())),("role",Value::String("inspector".into()))])),
   ("grants".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("grant".into())),("principal",Value::Id("dev:fixture-only".into()))])),
   ("reports".into(),row(&[("id",Value::Id("report".into())),("body",Value::String("private".into()))]))
  ],now).unwrap();
  let mut campaign=security::challenges::Campaign::default();security::challenges::operations(&b,&fixture,&mut campaign);
  for name in ["inspect", "legacyInspect"] {assert!(campaign.operations.contains_key(name),"{campaign:?}");}
  assert_eq!(campaign.violations.is_empty(),fixed,"{campaign:?}");
  let args=std::collections::BTreeMap::from([("report".into(),Value::Id("report".into())),("old".into(),Value::String("old caller".into()))]);
  let mut context=snapback4_core::Context::new("dev:fixture-only",args);context.now=Some(now);
  let demote=std::collections::BTreeMap::from([("who".into(),Value::Id("dev:fixture-only".into()))]);
  let mut operator=snapback4_core::Context::new("service:ops",demote);operator.now=Some(now);
  fixture.mutate("demote",None,operator).unwrap();
  for name in ["inspect", "legacyInspect"] {
   let observed=fixture.query(name,context.clone()).unwrap();
   assert_eq!(observed.data==Value::Null,fixed,"{name}: {observed:?}");
  }
 }
}
